--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Mission Analysis:**
This is an AGGREGATION-like mission but re-reading: "score increases by 1 for every robot outside the target region." The target region is the strip with BLUE walls and BLACK floor. So robots need to get to the BLACK floor strip near BLUE walls.

Wait - both strips have BLACK floors, the middle is GRAY. The target is the strip with BLUE walls. Robots can detect:
- Floor color (BLACK = in a strip, GRAY = in the middle)
- Wall color via camera (BLUE walls = target strip, GREEN walls = wrong strip)

**Strategy:**
1. If a robot sees BLUE walls → it's near or in the target strip → stay/aggregate there
2. If a robot sees GREEN walls → it's in the wrong strip → move away
3. If floor is GRAY → in the middle → move toward BLUE walls
4. If floor is BLACK → in a strip → check wall color

**Champion Analysis (score 22064):**
- State 0: GoAwayColor(cyan/6) → moves away from cyan. Transitions: neighbors>1, sees green(2), floor black
- State 1: Exploration (rwm=13, slow turns) → transitions based on neighbors, floor
- State 2: FlockCenter → transitions based on few neighbors
- State 3: Repulsion → many transitions

The champion uses GoAwayColor with color 6 (cyan). This is interesting. Let me think about what helps robots reach the BLUE strip.

**Better strategy:** 
- GoToColor BLUE (clr=3) to head toward blue walls
- GoAwayColor GREEN (clr=2) to flee green walls
- When on BLACK floor near BLUE walls, stop or aggregate

Let me design FSMs:

1. **Champion copy** (elitism)
2. **Exploitation of champion** - small parameter tweaks
3. **New topology** - 2-state: GoToColor(blue) as main, transition when sees green to GoAwayColor(green)
4. **3-state**: Explore → GoToColor(blue) when sees blue → Stop when on black floor
5. Various explorations

**Key insight:** Color 3 = blue, color 2 = green. GoToColor(blue, clr=3) should move robots toward blue walls = target.

Let me build 14 machines:

1. Champion (exact copy)
2. Champion variant: adjust probabilities slightly
3. Champion variant: change exploration rwm
4. 2-state: GoToColor(blue) + FlockCenter
5. 3-state: GoToColor(blue) → stay on black, flee green
6. 2-state: GoAwayColor(green) + Attraction
7. 3-state pure navigation
8. 2-state: Exploration → GoToColor(blue)
9. 4-state with blue-seeking
10. Simple GoToColor(blue) single state
11. 2-state repulsion+navigation
12. 3-state with floor detection
13. Champion variant 3
14. Exploration candidate

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Huge breakthrough!** The champion jumped from ~22064 to **5534** with this simple 2-state FSM:
- State 0: GoToColor(blue/3) → if floor is BLACK (in a strip) with p=0.9, go to state 1; if neighbors>5 with w=8, go to state 1
- State 1: Attraction(3.0) → if floor is GRAY go back to state 0; if neighbors<2 go back to state 0

This makes great sense: robots navigate toward blue walls, and once in the blue strip (BLACK floor), they aggregate via attraction.

**Key insight:** GoToColor(blue=3) drives robots to the target. Once on black floor or with enough neighbors, switch to Attraction to cluster. If floor becomes gray (left the strip) or isolated, go back to seeking blue.

**Strategy:**
1. Keep champion exact
2. Exploit champion: tune att gain, probabilities, neighbor thresholds
3. Try 3-state variants adding a "stay" state or intermediate
4. Try varying the GoToColor target and attraction parameters
5. The 4-state runner-up (6766) also uses GoToColor(blue)+FlockCenter+GoAwayColor(green) - exploit that too

**Exploitation of champion (score 5534):**
- Try att1=2.0, 4.0, 5.0
- Try p0x0=0.95, 0.85
- Try neighbor threshold 3,4,6,7
- Try w0x1=6,10,12
- Try p1x0=0.5,0.7,0.8
- Try p1x1 threshold 1,3

**Exploitation of 2nd place (6766):**
- Tune fc1, transition probs

**Exploration:**
- 3-state: GoToColor(blue) → Attraction → Stop when many neighbors
- 3-state adding GoAwayColor(green) as escape

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Champion analysis (score 4354):**
- State 0: GoToColor(blue/3) → transition to state 1 if floor BLACK (p=0.9) OR neighbors>6 (w=8)
- State 1: Attraction(2.5) → transition to state 0 if floor GRAY (p=0.7) OR neighbors<3 (w=10)

This is a clean 2-state FSM. The key parameters to tune:
- `att1`: currently 2.5, try 2.0, 3.0, 1.5
- `p0x0` (black floor → attract): 0.9, try 0.95, 1.0
- `p0x1` (neighbor threshold): 6, try 5, 7, 8
- `w0x1` (sigmoid steepness): 8, try 6, 10, 12
- `p1x0` (gray → seek blue): 0.7, try 0.8, 0.6
- `p1x1` (neighbor threshold): 3, try 2, 4
- `w1x1` (sigmoid): 10, try 8, 12

**Observations:**
- The 2-state topology dominates - 3/4-state variants performed worse
- Transitions pointing to state 0 (same state issue) - wait, in 2-state FSM, `--n0x0 0` means transition from state 0 to state 0... No! The destination D is indexed among OTHER states. With 2 states, D=0 from state 0 means state 1, and D=0 from state 1 means state 0. So this is correct.
- Adding a "sees green" transition to state 0 helped slightly (rank 3)
- Higher neighbor threshold (6 vs 5) with attraction 2.5 seems best

**Plan:**
1. Champion exact copy (elitism)
2-7. Fine-tune champion parameters
8-10. Add green-detection transition (like rank 3)
11-12. Try different attraction gains
13-14. Explore slightly different topologies

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Champion (4354) is holding steady.** The core structure is very clear:
- State 0: GoToColor(blue/3) → if BLACK floor (p=0.9) switch to attract; if neighbors>6 (w=8) switch to attract
- State 1: Attraction(2.5) → if GRAY floor (p=0.7) go back; if neighbors<3 (w=10) go back

**Key observations from round 4:**
- Champion unchanged at 4354
- Best new performer (4542): adds "sees green" transition in state 0 AND "sees blue" in state 1 - interesting enrichment
- p0x0=0.95 performed worse (5183) - 0.9 is better
- att=3.0 with p1x1=4 (4628) is competitive
- Adding a small black-floor escape (p=0.1) to state 1 helped slightly (4633)
- Neighbor threshold 7 (4651) vs 6 (4354) - 6 is better
- 3-state variants with GoAwayColor(green) performed worst - avoid

**Exploitation strategy:**
- Keep champion exact
- Try more combinations of the "sees green" + "sees blue" enrichment
- Try att=2.0, 1.5, 4.0 with the winning topology
- Try w0x1=6, 10, 12 with neighbor threshold 6
- Try p1x1=4 with att=2.5
- Try combining black-floor escape in state 1 with other tweaks
- Try w1x1=8 or 12

**Exploration:**
- Try FlockCenter instead of Attraction in state 1
- Try different neighbor thresholds 5, 8

# PROMPT SYSTEME

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
The score is MINIMISED.

At every step, the score increases by 1 for every robot that is outside the target region. The score does not depend on how close the robots are to each other.

The arena has two strips at opposite ends. The floor is BLACK in both strips and GRAY in between. The target region is the strip whose bordering walls are BLUE; the walls bordering the other strip are GREEN.

# 2. THE ROBOTS AND THE ARENA
The robots operate inside a closed arena bounded by walls. 23 e-puck robots start scattered around the centre of the arena, with random positions and orientations that change at every run. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.51 m: neighbour-count conditions only count robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).

# 3. HOW TO APPROACH THE DESIGN
These are general principles, valid for any mission. They are not a solution to this one.
- Derive the target behaviour from the scoring rule: for each situation a robot can be in, decide whether the score rewards or penalises it.
- Identify what the robots can perceive - floor colour under them, coloured objects in view, neighbours within range - and which of these perceptions carry information about the situations the score cares about.
- Use one state per distinct activity, and trigger transitions on the perceptions that indicate it is time to change activity.

# 4. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 5. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=6  Spiral        -> traces an expanding spiral
            requires --spd<I>  integer in [1..100]  (spiral speed)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=10  FlockCenter   -> moves toward the centre of the group
            requires --fc<I>  integer in [1..100]  (flocking gain)
  B=11  ZigZag        -> moves in a zigzag pattern
            requires --zfr<I>  integer in [1..100]  (zigzag frequency)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type. A state that reacts to several different situations is often stronger than a state with one transition, and random sampling produces that variety by default.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 6. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every state that exists must declare `--n<I>`, and every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.
4. Never point a transition at its own state.

# 7. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
